CfPBoK by Vadim Zaytsev

Software and Systems Modeling Body of Knowledge, CfP edition

2885 authors • 1445 publications • 3485 distinct keywords • 42 tags • 2958 taggings

Topics

  • S1Modelling Notations and Paradigms
    • S1AModelling Foundations 15
      • Models, abstraction, representation, ontology of models
      • Work whose primary contribution concerns what models are and how modelling represents, abstracts, describes, or explains systems. Includes foundational principles, classification schemes, modelling theory, ontological evaluation, and relationships between models and their referents. Use more specific language, metamodelling, or assurance tags when the contribution is mainly technical.
    • S1BConceptual, Data and Ontology Modelling 91
      • Conceptual modelling, information modelling, data modelling, knowledge modelling, ontologies
      • Work on conceptual schemas and semantic representations of information or knowledge, including entity-relationship and object-role approaches, database and information-system models, ontologies, knowledge graphs, and semantic interoperability.
    • S1CGeneral-Purpose Modelling Languages 320
      • UML, OCL, SysML, profiles, state machines, sequence diagrams, Petri nets
      • Work centred on a broadly applicable modelling language or notation: its constructs, profiles, semantics, expressiveness, comparison, standardisation, or use. Typical examples include UML, OCL, SysML, statecharts, sequence diagrams, activity models, and Petri nets.
    • S1DDomain-Specific Modelling Languages 101
      • DSL, DSML, domain-specific modelling, domain-specific notation
      • Work whose main artefact is a modelling language specialised for a domain, including domain analysis, language design, concrete and abstract syntax, semantics, and domain-specific tooling. Do not use merely because a DSL is used as a vehicle for a more general transformation, testing, or tool contribution.
  • S2Modelling Language Engineering and Semantics
    • S2AMetamodelling and Language Definition 106
      • Metamodels, metamodelling, metametamodels, MOF, powertypes, language definition, linguistic and ontological classification
      • Work on defining and structuring modelling languages through metamodels and metametamodels, including metamodel conformance, instantiation, typing across language-definition levels, MOF-based architectures, powertype patterns, and linguistic versus ontological classification.
    • S2BMultilevel Modelling and Deep Instantiation 22
      • Multilevel modelling, deep modelling, deep instantiation, potency, clabjects, multilevel typing
      • Work on modelling approaches that use more than two classification or instantiation levels, including deep instantiation, potency, clabjects, multilevel typing, and elements that act simultaneously as types and instances. This includes linguistic and ontological classification when these distinctions are used to organise or relate multiple modelling levels.
    • S2CVisual and Diagrammatic Modelling 30
      • Visual languages, graphical syntax, diagrammatic reasoning, notation, layout
      • Work where visual representation is central: graphical or diagrammatic languages, notation design, diagram layout, visual reasoning, graphical editors, and visualisation of model structure or behaviour.
    • S2DSemantics and Executable Models 67
      • Formal semantics, operational semantics, executable models, model execution, models at runtime
      • Work defining or realising the meaning and execution of models, including operational, denotational, translational, or trace semantics, as well as executable models, action semantics, interpretation, and models at runtime.
    • S2EMulti-Paradigm, Blended and Multi-Formalism Modelling 15
      • Multi-paradigm modelling, multi-formalism modelling, blended modelling, heterogeneous models, language composition, formalism integration
      • Work whose main contribution combines, coordinates, or relates multiple modelling languages, paradigms, notations, or formalisms within one approach or system. Includes multi-paradigm, multi-formalism, blended, and heterogeneous modelling, as well as language and formalism composition, semantic bridges, and coordinated use or execution of different representations.
  • S3Model Operations, Evolution and Tooling
    • S3AModel Transformation 151
      • Model transformation, model-to-model transformation, ATL, QVT, transformation languages, bidirectional transformation, transformation correctness
      • Work on transforming models at comparable abstraction levels, including model-to-model transformation, transformation languages such as ATL and QVT, transformation composition, bidirectional and incremental transformations, transformation synchronisation, and the analysis or verification of transformation correctness.
    • S3BGraph Transformation and Graph Grammars 60
      • Graph transformation, graph grammars, graph rewriting, rule-based transformation, DPO, SPO, critical-pair analysis
      • Work on graph transformation and graph grammars as computational or formal techniques, including graph-rewriting rules, rule application and matching, algebraic approaches such as double-pushout and single-pushout transformation, critical-pair and dependency analysis, and the formal properties of graph-transformation systems.
    • S3CGeneration, Reverse Engineering and Abstraction 60
      • Code generation, model-to-text, synthesis, reverse engineering, model extraction, low-code
      • Work that crosses abstraction levels: generating code or other implementation artefacts from models, synthesising models, recovering models from code or data, reverse engineering, abstraction, and model-based low-code generation.
    • S3DEvolution, Refactoring and Migration 59
      • Model evolution, metamodel evolution, co-evolution, refactoring, migration, differencing
      • Work on changing models, modelling languages, metamodels, and related artefacts over time, including evolution, co-evolution, refactoring, migration, versioning, differencing, and change impact.
    • S3EConsistency, Synchronisation and Traceability 71
      • Consistency management, model synchronisation, traceability, correspondence, triple graph grammar
      • Work establishing, checking, maintaining, or exploiting relationships among models and related artefacts: consistency management, synchronisation, correspondence rules, trace links, provenance, round-trip engineering, and multi-view integration.
    • S3FModel Management, Interchange and Repositories 49
      • Model management, megamodels, repositories, model indexing, interchange, interoperability, model queries
      • Work on storing, organising, querying, composing, exchanging, or integrating collections of models and metamodels, including model repositories, megamodels, model indexing and search, slicing, model interchange formats, and interoperability infrastructure.
    • S3GModelling Tools and Collaborative Environments 70
      • Modelling tools, workbenches, editors, tool integration, collaborative modelling, language workbenches
      • Work whose main contribution is an environment for creating, editing, executing, integrating, or collaboratively managing models and modelling languages. Includes modelling workbenches, editors, tool integration frameworks, collaborative environments, and generated tool support.
  • S4Model Analysis, Assurance and Quality
    • S4AFormal Verification and Model Checking 174
      • Formal methods, formal specification, model checking, theorem proving, temporal logic, refinement
      • Work applying mathematically rigorous specification or verification to models and modelled systems, including model checking, theorem proving, temporal or modal logic, refinement proofs, equivalence checking, and verified transformations.
    • S4BValidation, Constraint Solving and Static Analysis 64
      • Model validation, consistency checking, constraint solving, SAT/SMT, static analysis, snapshot generation
      • Work on determining whether a model is well-formed, satisfiable, complete, or compliant using validation rules, constraint solving, static analysis, invariant checking, snapshot or instance generation, and diagnostic analysis.
    • S4CModel-Based Testing and Test Generation 59
      • Model-based testing, model testing, test generation, mutation testing, test coverage
      • Work using models to derive, prioritise, execute, or assess tests, or testing models and model transformations themselves. Includes model-based testing, test-case and oracle generation, mutation testing, test adequacy and coverage, and regression testing of modelled systems.
    • S4DModel Quality, Metrics and Smells 50
      • Model quality, metrics, maintainability, understandability, smells, quality assessment
      • Work defining, measuring, predicting, or improving structural or cognitive quality of models and modelling artefacts, including metrics, quality frameworks, smells and antipatterns, maintainability, comprehensibility, and quality assessment.
    • S4EPerformance and Scalability Modelling 53
      • Performance models, performance prediction, scalability, workload models, queueing networks, optimisation
      • Work on modelling, predicting, or optimising performance, resource consumption, throughput, latency, or scalability of software and systems. Includes performance annotations, workload models, simulation-based performance analysis, queueing networks, and scalability of modelling infrastructure.
    • S4FDependability, Safety and Reliability 56
      • Dependability, safety, reliability, availability, fault tolerance, hazard analysis, uncertainty
      • Work on dependable and critical systems using models, including safety, reliability, availability, resilience, fault tolerance, fault injection, hazard and risk analysis, and uncertainty that affects assurance.
    • S4GSecurity, Privacy and Policy Modelling 61
      • Security modelling, privacy, threat modelling, access control, policy, trust
      • Work where models represent, analyse, enforce, or verify security, privacy, trust, access-control, compliance, or policy concerns. Includes threat modelling, security requirements, attack and misuse models, privacy models, and formal verification of security policies.
    • S4HOptimisation, Design-Space Exploration and Search-Based Engineering 26
      • Optimisation, design-space exploration, search-based engineering, multi-objective optimisation, trade-off analysis, evolutionary algorithms
      • Work using models as search, decision, or configuration spaces to identify preferred designs, architectures, transformations, or parameter settings. Includes design-space and architecture trade-space exploration, search-based software engineering, evolutionary and other metaheuristic optimisation, multi-objective optimisation, Pareto analysis, automated design synthesis, and model-driven optimisation.
  • S5Software, Process and Enterprise Modelling
    • S5ARequirements, Scenarios and Use Cases 77
      • Requirements engineering, use cases, scenarios, goals, problem frames, requirements traceability
      • Work on eliciting, representing, analysing, negotiating, or operationalising requirements using models, including goals, scenarios, use cases, problem frames, requirements variability, and links from requirements to design.
    • S5BSoftware Architecture, Components and Services 82
      • Software architecture, architecture description, components, connectors, services, SOA, architectural styles
      • Work on architectural models of software-intensive systems, including components and connectors, architectural styles and viewpoints, service-oriented and microservice architectures, architecture description languages, architecture refinement, and separation of concerns.
    • S5CVariability, Features and Product Lines 64
      • Software product lines, feature models, variability, configuration, product families
      • Work on modelling and engineering families of systems, including feature models, variability and configuration, product-line architectures, product derivation, feature interactions, and evolution or testing of product lines.
    • S5DBusiness Process Modelling and Management 134
      • BPM, BPMN, workflow, business rules, process modelling, process compliance
      • Work on representing, analysing, executing, or managing business processes and workflows, including BPMN and other process languages, workflow patterns, business rules, process compliance, process families, and process-aware information systems.
    • S5EProcess Mining and Event-Log Analytics 40
      • Process mining, event logs, process discovery, conformance checking, predictive process monitoring
      • Work deriving or analysing process models from observed behaviour, including event-log preparation, process discovery, conformance checking, organisational and resource mining, predictive monitoring, deviance analysis, and process visual analytics.
    • S5FEnterprise and Organisational Modelling 51
      • Enterprise architecture, enterprise modelling, ArchiMate, capability modelling, organisational modelling
      • Work on modelling enterprises and organisations, including enterprise architecture, ArchiMate, capabilities, goals, governance, organisational structures, business-IT alignment, and transformation of enterprise models.
    • S5GAdaptive, Context-Aware and Autonomous Systems 38
      • Self-adaptive systems, context awareness, autonomic systems, dynamic reconfiguration, agents
      • Work on models for systems that adapt or act autonomously, including context-aware applications, self-adaptive and autonomic systems, runtime reconfiguration, feedback loops, multi-agent systems, and planning or control based on models at runtime.
    • S5HSoftware Development Processes, Methods and Lifecycle 77
      • Software processes, development methods, method engineering, lifecycle, Agile, Scrum, DevOps, RUP, PLM, ALM
      • Work on modelling, defining, tailoring, enacting, assessing, or improving software and systems development processes, methods, and lifecycle practices. Includes software-process modelling, method and situational method engineering, process frameworks such as RUP, Agile and Scrum practices, DevOps, project and lifecycle management, PLM and ALM, and coordination of development activities.
  • S6System Domains and Computational Paradigms
    • S6AEmbedded, Real-Time, IoT and Cyber-Physical Systems 117
      • Embedded systems, real-time systems, CPS, IoT, automotive, mechatronics
      • Work applying modelling to embedded, reactive, real-time, cyber-physical, Internet-of-Things, automotive, avionics, railway, or mechatronic systems.
    • S6BSimulation, Co-Simulation and Digital Twins 70
      • Simulation, co-simulation, digital twin, virtual prototyping, hybrid simulation
      • Work where simulation is the main engineering mechanism, including discrete-event or continuous simulation, co-simulation, virtual prototyping, executable simulation models, digital twins, and synchronisation between operational assets and their digital representations.
    • S6CAI/ML and Data-Driven Modelling 74
      • Artificial intelligence, machine learning, deep learning, NLP, generative AI, learning-based modelling
      • Work at the intersection of modelling and data-driven intelligence: AI or machine learning for creating, completing, transforming, analysing, or executing models, as well as model-based engineering of AI systems. Includes neural models, natural-language processing, learned representations, and generative AI.
    • S6DWeb, Cloud, Distributed and Service-Based Systems 69
      • Web applications, cloud computing, distributed systems, web services, microservices, blockchain
      • Work applying modelling to web, cloud, distributed, mobile, service-based, or blockchain systems.
    • S6EIndustrial, Manufacturing and Robotics Systems 22
      • Industrial systems, manufacturing, Industry 4.0, production systems, robotics, automation
      • Work applying modelling to industrial production, manufacturing, Industry 4.0, robotics, automation, or engineering operations.
  • S7Evidence, Practice and Human Factors
    • S7AEmpirical Evaluation and Experiments 67
      • Empirical study, controlled experiment, benchmark, evaluation, corpus study, replication
      • Work whose primary contribution is evidence from controlled or observational empirical research, benchmarks, corpus studies, qualitative or quantitative evaluations, replications, or comparative experiments. Do not use merely because a technical paper contains a small illustrative case.
    • S7BSurveys, Reviews and Secondary Studies 60
      • Survey, systematic literature review, mapping study, meta-review, bibliometric study, taxonomy
      • Work synthesising existing knowledge rather than proposing a primary technical method, including surveys, systematic literature reviews, systematic mappings, tertiary studies, bibliometric analyses, taxonomies, and research landscape studies.
    • S7CIndustrial Practice and Experience Reports 32
      • Industrial case study, experience report, practice, technology transfer, lessons learned
      • Work centred on real-world adoption, industrial deployment, long-running practice, technology transfer, or lessons learned from applying modelling in organisations. A paper may also receive the relevant technical and application-domain tags.
    • S7DHuman Factors, Education and Adoption 43
      • Usability, human factors, collaborative practice, education, teaching, adoption, developer experience
      • Work whose main question concerns people learning, reading, creating, or adopting models and modelling tools, including usability, comprehension, cognitive aspects, collaborative practice, education, training, developer experience, and organisational adoption.
    • S7EResearch Methodology, Community and Meta-Science 11
      • Research methodology, research design, evaluation methodology, scientific practice, research agendas, roadmaps, grand challenges, community studies
      • Work whose subject is the conduct, evaluation, organisation, communication, or future direction of modelling research itself. Includes methodological guidance, research and evaluation frameworks, criteria for contributions and evidence, research agendas, roadmaps, grand challenges, community and disciplinary reflection, and analyses of scientific practice. Use S7A for empirical evaluation of a technical or behavioural phenomenon, and S7B for surveys, literature reviews, bibliometric studies, or taxonomies that synthesise an existing research area.
